---
title: "google_container_cluster"
description: "`google_container_cluster` is a Google Kubernetes Engine cluster..."
---

`google_container_cluster` is a Google Kubernetes Engine cluster, the managed Kubernetes control plane and node infrastructure that workload(s) are deployed to.

## Example

<Tabs>
  <Tab title="Go">
    ```go
    package main

    import (
    	ubx "github.com/ubiquex/ubx-sdk-go/runtime"
    	container "github.com/ubiquex/ubx-sdk-google/sdk/go/google/container"
    )

    func main() {
    	ubx.Main(ubx.Stack("example", func() {
    		ubx.Intent(ubx.IntentInfo{Summary: "example own cluster"})

    		ubx.Resource(container.Cluster, "example", container.ClusterConfig{
    			Cluster:   map[string]any{"name": "example-name"},
    			Parent:    "example",
    			ProjectId: "example",
    		})
    	}))
    }
    ```
  </Tab>
  <Tab title="TypeScript">
    ```typescript
    import { intent, resource, stack } from "@ubx/sdk";
    import { Cluster } from "@ubx/sdk-google/google/container/cluster";

    export default stack("example", () => {
      intent({ summary: "example own cluster" });

      resource(Cluster, "example", {
        cluster: { name: "example-name" },
        parent: "example",
        projectId: "example",
      });
    });
    ```
  </Tab>
  <Tab title="Python">
    ```python
    import ubx_sdk as ubx
    from ubx.google.container.cluster import Cluster, ClusterConfig

    def describe():
        ubx.intent("example own cluster")

        ubx.resource(Cluster, "example", ClusterConfig(
            cluster={"name": "example-name"},
            parent="example",
            project_id="example",
        ))

    if __name__ == "__main__":
        ubx.run("example", describe)
    ```
  </Tab>
  <Tab title="Markdown">
    ```
    Create google_container_cluster, with cluster a set value, parent
    example, project id example.
    ```
  </Tab>
</Tabs>

<ResponseField name="cluster" type="object">
  A Google Kubernetes Engine cluster. Optional.
  <Expandable title="properties">
    <ResponseField name="addons_config" type="object">

          Configuration options for the horizontal pod autoscaling feature, which increases or decreases the number of replica pods a replication controller has based on the resource usage of the existing pods. Optional.

              Whether the Horizontal Pod Autoscaling feature is enabled in the cluster. When enabled, it ensures that metrics are collected into Stackdriver Monitoring. Optional.

        <ResponseField name="http_load_balancing" type="object">

            <ResponseField name="disabled" type="bool">
              Whether the HTTP Load Balancing controller is enabled in the cluster. When enabled, it runs a small pod in the cluster that manages the load balancers. Optional.

        <ResponseField name="kubernetes_dashboard" type="object">
          Configuration for the Kubernetes Dashboard. Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="disabled" type="bool">
              Whether the Kubernetes Dashboard is enabled for this cluster. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="lustre_csi_driver_config" type="object">
          Configuration for the Lustre CSI driver. Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="disable_multi_nic" type="bool">
              When set to true, this disables multi-NIC support for the Lustre CSI driver. By default, GKE enables multi-NIC support, which allows the Lustre CSI driver to automatically detect and configure all suitable network interfaces on a node to maximize I/O performance for demanding workloads. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="enable_legacy_lustre_port" type="bool">
              If set to true, the Lustre CSI driver will install Lustre kernel modules using port 6988. This serves as a workaround for a port conflict with the gke-metadata-server. This field is required ONLY under the following conditions: 1. The GKE node version is older than 1.33.2-gke.4655000. 2. You're connecting to a Lustre instance that has the 'gke-support-enabled' flag. Deprecated: This flag is no longer required as of GKE node version 1.33.2-gke.4655000, unless you are connecting to a Lustre instance that has the `gke-support-enabled` flag. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="enabled" type="bool">
              Whether the Lustre CSI driver is enabled for this cluster. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="network_policy_config" type="object">

    <ResponseField name="autopilot" type="object">
      Autopilot is the configuration for Autopilot settings on the cluster. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="cluster_policy_config" type="object">
          ClusterPolicyConfig stores the configuration for cluster wide policies. Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="no_standard_node_pools" type="bool">
              Denotes preventing standard node pools and requiring only autopilot node pools. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="no_system_impersonation" type="bool">
              Denotes preventing impersonation and CSRs for GKE System users. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="no_system_mutation" type="bool">
              Denotes that preventing creation and mutation of resources in GKE managed namespaces and cluster-scoped GKE managed resources . Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="no_unsafe_webhooks" type="bool">
              Denotes preventing unsafe webhooks. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="enabled" type="bool">
          Enable Autopilot Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="privileged_admission_config" type="object">
          PrivilegedAdmissionConfig stores the list of authorized allowlist paths for the cluster. Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="allowlist_paths" type="list(string)">
              The customer allowlist Cloud Storage paths for the cluster. These paths are used with the `--autopilot-privileged-admission` flag to authorize privileged workloads in Autopilot clusters. Paths can be GKE-owned, in the format `gke:////`, or customer-owned, in the format `gs:///`. Wildcards (`*`) are supported to authorize all allowlists under specific paths or directories. Example: `gs://my-bucket/*` will authorize all allowlists under the `my-bucket` bucket. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="workload_policy_config" type="object">
          WorkloadPolicyConfig is the configuration related to GCW workload policy Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="allow_net_admin" type="bool">
              If true, workloads can use NET_ADMIN capability. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="autopilot_compatibility_auditing_enabled" type="bool">
              If true, enables the GCW Auditor that audits workloads on standard clusters. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="autoscaling" type="object">
      ClusterAutoscaling contains global, per-cluster information required by Cluster Autoscaler to automatically adjust the size of the cluster and create/delete node pools based on the current needs. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="autopilot_general_profile" type="string">
          Autopilot general profile for the cluster, which defines the configuration for the cluster. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="autoprovisioning_locations" type="list(string)">
          The list of Google Compute Engine [zones](https://cloud.google.com/compute/docs/zones#available) in which the NodePool's nodes can be created by NAP. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="autoprovisioning_node_pool_defaults" type="object">
          AutoprovisioningNodePoolDefaults contains defaults for a node pool created by NAP. Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="boot_disk_kms_key" type="string">
              The Customer Managed Encryption Key used to encrypt the boot disk attached to each node in the node pool. This should be of the form projects/[KEY_PROJECT_ID]/locations/[LOCATION]/keyRings/[RING_NAME]/cryptoKeys/[KEY_NAME]. For more information about protecting resources with Cloud KMS Keys please see: https://cloud.google.com/compute/docs/disks/customer-managed-encryption Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="disk_size_gb" type="number">
              Size of the disk attached to each node, specified in GB. The smallest allowed disk size is 10GB. If unspecified, the default disk size is 100GB. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="disk_type" type="string">
              Type of the disk attached to each node (e.g. 'pd-standard', 'pd-ssd' or 'pd-balanced') If unspecified, the default disk type is 'pd-standard' Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="image_type" type="string">
              The image type to use for NAP created node. Please see https://cloud.google.com/kubernetes-engine/docs/concepts/node-images for available image types. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="insecure_kubelet_readonly_port_enabled" type="bool">
              DEPRECATED. Use NodePoolAutoConfig.NodeKubeletConfig instead. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="management" type="object">
              NodeManagement defines the set of node management services turned on for the node pool. Optional.
              <Expandable title="properties">
                <ResponseField name="auto_repair" type="bool">
                  A flag that specifies whether the node auto-repair is enabled for the node pool. If enabled, the nodes in this node pool will be monitored and, if they fail health checks too many times, an automatic repair action will be triggered. Optional.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="auto_upgrade" type="bool">
                  A flag that specifies whether node auto-upgrade is enabled for the node pool. If enabled, node auto-upgrade helps keep the nodes in your node pool up to date with the latest release version of Kubernetes. Optional.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="upgrade_options" type="object">
                  AutoUpgradeOptions defines the set of options for the user to control how the Auto Upgrades will proceed. Optional.
                  <Expandable title="properties">
                    <ResponseField name="auto_upgrade_start_time" type="string">
                      Output only. This field is set when upgrades are about to commence with the approximate start time for the upgrades, in [RFC3339](https://www.ietf.org/rfc/rfc3339.txt) text format. Computed by Google Cloud after `ship`.
                    </ResponseField>
                    <ResponseField name="description" type="string">
                      Output only. This field is set when upgrades are about to commence with the description of the upgrade. Computed by Google Cloud after `ship`.
                    </ResponseField>
                  </Expandable>
                </ResponseField>
              </Expandable>
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="min_cpu_platform" type="string">
              Deprecated. Minimum CPU platform to be used for NAP created node pools. The instance may be scheduled on the specified or newer CPU platform. Applicable values are the friendly names of CPU platforms, such as minCpuPlatform: Intel Haswell or minCpuPlatform: Intel Sandy Bridge. For more information, read [how to specify min CPU platform](https://cloud.google.com/compute/docs/instances/specify-min-cpu-platform). This field is deprecated, min_cpu_platform should be specified using `cloud.google.com/requested-min-cpu-platform` label selector on the pod. To unset the min cpu platform field pass "automatic" as field value. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="oauth_scopes" type="list(string)">
              Scopes that are used by NAP when creating node pools. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="service_account" type="string">
              The Google Cloud Platform Service Account to be used by the node VMs. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="shielded_instance_config" type="object">
              A set of Shielded Instance options. Optional.
              <Expandable title="properties">
                <ResponseField name="enable_integrity_monitoring" type="bool">
                  Defines whether the instance has integrity monitoring enabled. Enables monitoring and attestation of the boot integrity of the instance. The attestation is performed against the integrity policy baseline. This baseline is initially derived from the implicitly trusted boot image when the instance is created. Optional.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="enable_secure_boot" type="bool">
                  Defines whether the instance has Secure Boot enabled. Secure Boot helps ensure that the system only runs authentic software by verifying the digital signature of all boot components, and halting the boot process if signature verification fails. Optional.
                </ResponseField>
              </Expandable>
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="upgrade_settings" type="object">
              These upgrade settings control the level of parallelism and the level of disruption caused by an upgrade. maxUnavailable controls the number of nodes that can be simultaneously unavailable. maxSurge controls the number of additional nodes that can be added to the node pool temporarily for the time of the upgrade to increase the number of available nodes. (maxUnavailable + maxSurge) determines the level of parallelism (how many nodes are being upgraded at the same time). Note: upgrades inevitably introduce some disruption since workloads need to be moved from old nodes to new, upgraded ones. Even if maxUnavailable=0, this holds true. (Disruption stays within the limits of PodDisruptionBudget, if it is configured.) Consider a hypothetical node pool with 5 nodes having maxSurge=2, maxUnavailable=1. This means the upgrade process upgrades 3 nodes simultaneously. It creates 2 additional (upgraded) nodes, then it brings down 3 old (not yet upgraded) nodes at the same time. This ensures that there are always at least 4 nodes available. These upgrade settings configure the upgrade strategy for the node pool. Use strategy to switch between the strategies applied to the node pool. If the strategy is ROLLING, use max_surge and max_unavailable to control the level of parallelism and the level of disruption caused by upgrade. 1. maxSurge controls the number of additional nodes that can be added to the node pool temporarily for the time of the upgrade to increase the number of available nodes. 2. maxUnavailable controls the number of nodes that can be simultaneously unavailable. 3. (maxUnavailable + maxSurge) determines the level of parallelism (how many nodes are being upgraded at the same time). If the strategy is BLUE_GREEN, use blue_green_settings to configure the blue-green upgrade related settings. 1. standard_rollout_policy is the default policy. The policy is used to control the way blue pool gets drained. The draining is executed in the batch mode. The batch size could be specified as either percentage of the node pool size or the number of nodes. batch_soak_duration is the soak time after each batch gets drained. 2. node_pool_soak_duration is the soak time after all blue nodes are drained. After this period, the blue pool nodes will be deleted. Optional.
              <Expandable title="properties">
                <ResponseField name="blue_green_settings" type="object">
                  Settings for blue-green upgrade. Optional.
                  <Expandable title="properties">
                    <ResponseField name="autoscaled_rollout_policy" type="object">
                      Autoscaled rollout policy utilizes the cluster autoscaler during blue-green upgrade to scale both the blue and green pools. Optional.
                      <Expandable title="properties">
                        <ResponseField name="wait_for_drain_duration" type="string">
                          Optional. Time to wait after cordoning the blue pool before draining the nodes. Defaults to 3 days. The value can be set between 0 and 7 days, inclusive. Optional.
                        </ResponseField>
                      </Expandable>
                    </ResponseField>
                    <ResponseField name="node_pool_soak_duration" type="string">
                      Time needed after draining entire blue pool. After this period, blue pool will be cleaned up. Optional.
                    </ResponseField>
                    <ResponseField name="standard_rollout_policy" type="object">
                      Standard rollout policy is the default policy for blue-green. Optional.
                      <Expandable title="properties">
                        <ResponseField name="batch_node_count" type="number">
                          Number of blue nodes to drain in a batch. Optional.
                        </ResponseField>
                        <ResponseField name="batch_percentage" type="number">
                          Percentage of the blue pool nodes to drain in a batch. The range of this field should be (0.0, 1.0]. Optional.
                        </ResponseField>
                        <ResponseField name="batch_soak_duration" type="string">
                          Soak time after each batch gets drained. Default to zero. Optional.
                        </ResponseField>
                      </Expandable>
                    </ResponseField>
                  </Expandable>
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="max_surge" type="number">
                  The maximum number of nodes that can be created beyond the current size of the node pool during the upgrade process. Optional.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="max_unavailable" type="number">
                  The maximum number of nodes that can be simultaneously unavailable during the upgrade process. A node is considered available if its status is Ready. Optional.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="strategy" type="string">
                  Update strategy of the node pool. Optional.
                </ResponseField>
              </Expandable>
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="autoscaling_profile" type="string">
          Defines autoscaling behaviour. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="default_compute_class_config" type="object">
          DefaultComputeClassConfig defines default compute class configuration. Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="enabled" type="bool">
              Enables default compute class. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="enable_node_autoprovisioning" type="bool">
          Enables automatic node pool creation and deletion. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="resource_limits" type="list(object)">
          Contains global constraints regarding minimum and maximum amount of resources in the cluster. Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="maximum" type="string">
              The largest value a `number`/`integer`-typed value may take. Optional; if omitted, computed by Google Cloud. **(AI-inferred)**
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="minimum" type="string">
              The smallest value a `number`/`integer`-typed value may take. Optional; if omitted, computed by Google Cloud. **(AI-inferred)**
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="resource_type" type="string">
              Which kind of resource this applies to. Optional; if omitted, computed by Google Cloud. **(AI-inferred)**
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="binary_authorization" type="object">
      Configuration for Binary Authorization. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="enabled" type="bool">
          This field is deprecated. Leave this unset and instead configure BinaryAuthorization using evaluation_mode. If evaluation_mode is set to anything other than EVALUATION_MODE_UNSPECIFIED, this field is ignored. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="evaluation_mode" type="string">
          Mode of operation for binauthz policy evaluation. If unspecified, defaults to DISABLED. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="cluster_ipv4_cidr" type="string">
      The IP address range of the container pods in this cluster, in [CIDR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classless_Inter-Domain_Routing) notation (e.g. `10.96.0.0/14`). Leave blank to have one automatically chosen or specify a `/14` block in `10.0.0.0/8`. Optional.
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="compliance_posture_config" type="object">
      Deprecated: Compliance Posture is no longer supported. For more details, see https://cloud.google.com/kubernetes-engine/docs/deprecations/posture-management-deprecation. CompliancePostureConfig defines the settings needed to enable/disable features for the Compliance Posture. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="compliance_standards" type="list(object)">
          List of enabled compliance standards. Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="standard" type="string">
              Configuration for the standard (non-Autopilot) cluster mode. Optional; if omitted, computed by Google Cloud. **(AI-inferred)**
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="mode" type="string">
          Defines the enablement mode for Compliance Posture. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="conditions" type="list(object)">

The version can be upgraded over time; such upgrades are reflected in currentMasterVersion and currentNodeVersion. Users may specify either explicit versions offered by Kubernetes Engine or version aliases, which have the following behavior: - "latest": picks the highest valid Kubernetes version - "1.X": picks the highest valid patch+gke.N patch in the 1.X version - "1.X.Y": picks the highest valid gke.N patch in the 1.X.Y version - "1.X.Y-gke.N": picks an explicit Kubernetes version - "","-": picks the default Kubernetes version Optional.

Do not use this and a node_pool at the same time. This field is deprecated, use node_pool.initial_node_count instead. Optional.

              Output only. The last time a minor version upgrade was performed on the control plane. Computed by Google Cloud after `ship`.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="minor_version_disruption_interval" type="string">
              Optional. The minimum duration between two minor version upgrades of the control plane. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="patch_version_disruption_interval" type="string">
              Optional. The minimum duration between two patch version upgrades of the control plane. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="resource_version" type="string">
          A hash identifying the version of this policy, so that updates to fields of the policy won't accidentally undo intermediate changes (and so that users of the API unaware of some fields won't accidentally remove other fields). Make a `get()` request to the cluster to get the current resource version and include it with requests to set the policy. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="window" type="object">
          MaintenanceWindow defines the maintenance window to be used for the cluster. Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="daily_maintenance_window" type="object">
              Time window specified for daily maintenance operations. Optional.
              <Expandable title="properties">
                <ResponseField name="duration" type="string">
                  Output only. Duration of the time window, automatically chosen to be smallest possible in the given scenario. Duration will be in [RFC3339](https://www.ietf.org/rfc/rfc3339.txt) format "PTnHnMnS". Computed by Google Cloud after `ship`.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="start_time" type="string">
                  Time within the maintenance window to start the maintenance operations. Time format should be in [RFC3339](https://www.ietf.org/rfc/rfc3339.txt) format "HH:MM", where HH : [00-23] and MM : [00-59] GMT. Optional.
                </ResponseField>
              </Expandable>
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="maintenance_exclusions" type="map(object)">
              Exceptions to maintenance window. Non-emergency maintenance should not occur in these windows. Optional.
              <Expandable title="properties">
                <ResponseField name="end_time" type="string">
                  When this operation, run, or window ended. Optional; if omitted, computed by Google Cloud. **(AI-inferred)**
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="maintenance_exclusion_options" type="object">
                  Configuration for a maintenance exclusion window, e.g. its own scope. Optional; if omitted, computed by Google Cloud. **(AI-inferred)**
                  <Expandable title="properties">
                    <ResponseField name="end_time_behavior" type="string">
                      What happens once this maintenance exclusion window's own end time is reached. Optional; if omitted, computed by Google Cloud. **(AI-inferred)**
                    </ResponseField>
                    <ResponseField name="scope" type="string">
                      What this setting applies to. Optional; if omitted, computed by Google Cloud. **(AI-inferred)**
                    </ResponseField>
                  </Expandable>
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="start_time" type="string">
                  When this operation, run, or window started. Optional; if omitted, computed by Google Cloud. **(AI-inferred)**
                </ResponseField>
              </Expandable>
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="recurring_maintenance_window" type="object">
              Represents an arbitrary window of time that recurs. Will replace RecurringTimeWindow. Optional.
              <Expandable title="properties">
                <ResponseField name="delay_until" type="object">
                  Represents a whole or partial calendar date, such as a birthday. The time of day and time zone are either specified elsewhere or are insignificant. The date is relative to the Gregorian Calendar. This can represent one of the following: * A full date, with non-zero year, month, and day values. * A month and day, with a zero year (for example, an anniversary). * A year on its own, with a zero month and a zero day. * A year and month, with a zero day (for example, a credit card expiration date). Related types: * google.type.TimeOfDay * google.type.DateTime * google.protobuf.Timestamp Optional.
                  <Expandable title="properties">
                    <ResponseField name="day" type="number">
                      Day of a month. Must be from 1 to 31 and valid for the year and month, or 0 to specify a year by itself or a year and month where the day isn't significant. Optional.
                    </ResponseField>
                    <ResponseField name="month" type="number">
                      Month of a year. Must be from 1 to 12, or 0 to specify a year without a month and day. Optional.
                    </ResponseField>
                    <ResponseField name="year" type="number">
                      Year of the date. Must be from 1 to 9999, or 0 to specify a date without a year. Optional.
                    </ResponseField>
                  </Expandable>
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="recurrence" type="string">
                  Required. An RRULE (https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc5545#section-3.8.5.3) for how this window recurs. For example, to have something repeat every weekday, you'd use: `FREQ=WEEKLY;BYDAY=MO,TU,WE,TH,FR` To repeat some window daily (equivalent to the DailyMaintenanceWindow): `FREQ=DAILY` For the first weekend of every month: `FREQ=MONTHLY;BYSETPOS=1;BYDAY=SA,SU` The FREQ values of HOURLY, MINUTELY, and SECONDLY are not supported. Optional.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="window_duration" type="string">
                  Required. Duration of the window. Optional.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="window_start_time" type="object">
                  Represents a time of day. The date and time zone are either not significant or are specified elsewhere. An API may choose to allow leap seconds. Related types are google.type.Date and `google.protobuf.Timestamp`. Optional.
                  <Expandable title="properties">
                    <ResponseField name="hours" type="number">
                      Hours of a day in 24 hour format. Must be greater than or equal to 0 and typically must be less than or equal to 23. An API may choose to allow the value "24:00:00" for scenarios like business closing time. Optional.
                    </ResponseField>
                    <ResponseField name="minutes" type="number">
                      Minutes of an hour. Must be greater than or equal to 0 and less than or equal to 59. Optional.
                    </ResponseField>
                    <ResponseField name="nanos" type="number">
                      Fractions of seconds, in nanoseconds. Must be greater than or equal to 0 and less than or equal to 999,999,999. Optional.
                    </ResponseField>
                    <ResponseField name="seconds" type="number">
                      Seconds of a minute. Must be greater than or equal to 0 and typically must be less than or equal to 59. An API may allow the value 60 if it allows leap-seconds. Optional.
                    </ResponseField>
                  </Expandable>
                </ResponseField>
              </Expandable>
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="recurring_window" type="object">
              Represents an arbitrary window of time that recurs. Optional.
              <Expandable title="properties">
                <ResponseField name="recurrence" type="string">
                  An RRULE (https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc5545#section-3.8.5.3) for how this window recurs. They go on for the span of time between the start and end time. For example, to have something repeat every weekday, you'd use: `FREQ=WEEKLY;BYDAY=MO,TU,WE,TH,FR` To repeat some window daily (equivalent to the DailyMaintenanceWindow): `FREQ=DAILY` For the first weekend of every month: `FREQ=MONTHLY;BYSETPOS=1;BYDAY=SA,SU` This specifies how frequently the window starts. Eg, if you wanted to have a 9-5 UTC-4 window every weekday, you'd use something like: ``` start time = 2019-01-01T09:00:00-0400 end time = 2019-01-01T17:00:00-0400 recurrence = FREQ=WEEKLY;BYDAY=MO,TU,WE,TH,FR ``` Windows can span multiple days. Eg, to make the window encompass every weekend from midnight Saturday till the last minute of Sunday UTC: ``` start time = 2019-01-05T00:00:00Z end time = 2019-01-07T23:59:00Z recurrence = FREQ=WEEKLY;BYDAY=SA ``` Note the start and end time's specific dates are largely arbitrary except to specify duration of the window and when it first starts. The FREQ values of HOURLY, MINUTELY, and SECONDLY are not supported. Optional.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="window" type="object">
                  Represents an arbitrary window of time. Optional.
                  <Expandable title="properties">
                    <Note>`window` recurs here (its own type contains itself); see the `window` field's own properties above for the repeating pattern.</Note>
                  </Expandable>
                </ResponseField>
              </Expandable>
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="managed_machine_learning_diagnostics_config" type="object">
      ManagedMachineLearningDiagnosticsConfig is the configuration for the GKE Managed Machine Learning Diagnostics pipeline. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="enabled" type="bool">
          Enable/Disable Managed Machine Learning Diagnostics. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="managed_opentelemetry_config" type="object">
      ManagedOpenTelemetryConfig is the configuration for the GKE Managed OpenTelemetry pipeline. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="scope" type="string">
          Scope of the Managed OpenTelemetry pipeline. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="master_auth" type="object">
      The authentication information for accessing the master endpoint. Authentication can be done using HTTP basic auth or using client certificates. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="client_certificate" type="string">
          Output only. Base64-encoded public certificate used by clients to authenticate to the cluster endpoint. Issued only if client_certificate_config is set. Computed by Google Cloud after `ship`.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="client_certificate_config" type="object">
          Configuration for client certificates on the cluster. Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="issue_client_certificate" type="bool">
              Issue a client certificate. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="client_key" type="string">
          Output only. Base64-encoded private key used by clients to authenticate to the cluster endpoint. Computed by Google Cloud after `ship`.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="cluster_ca_certificate" type="string">
          Output only. Base64-encoded public certificate that is the root of trust for the cluster. Computed by Google Cloud after `ship`.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="password" type="string">
          The password to use for HTTP basic authentication to the master endpoint. Because the master endpoint is open to the Internet, you should create a strong password. If a password is provided for cluster creation, username must be non-empty. Warning: basic authentication is deprecated, and will be removed in GKE control plane versions 1.19 and newer. For a list of recommended authentication methods, see: https://cloud.google.com/kubernetes-engine/docs/how-to/api-server-authentication Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="username" type="string">
          The username to use for HTTP basic authentication to the master endpoint. For clusters v1.6.0 and later, basic authentication can be disabled by leaving username unspecified (or setting it to the empty string). Warning: basic authentication is deprecated, and will be removed in GKE control plane versions 1.19 and newer. For a list of recommended authentication methods, see: https://cloud.google.com/kubernetes-engine/docs/how-to/api-server-authentication Optional.
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="master_authorized_networks_config" type="object">
      Configuration options for the master authorized networks feature. Enabled master authorized networks will disallow all external traffic to access Kubernetes master through HTTPS except traffic from the given CIDR blocks, Google Compute Engine Public IPs and Google Prod IPs. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="cidr_blocks" type="list(object)">
          cidr_blocks define up to 50 external networks that could access Kubernetes master through HTTPS. Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="cidr_block" type="string">
              An IP address range in CIDR notation. Optional; if omitted, computed by Google Cloud. **(AI-inferred)**
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="display_name" type="string">
              A mutable, user-settable, human-readable name for this resource, shown in the console UI. Not required to be unique. Optional; if omitted, computed by Google Cloud. **(AI-inferred)**
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="enabled" type="bool">
          Whether or not master authorized networks is enabled. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="gcp_public_cidrs_access_enabled" type="bool">
          Whether master is accessible via Google Compute Engine Public IP addresses. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="private_endpoint_enforcement_enabled" type="bool">
          Whether master authorized networks is enforced on private endpoint or not. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="mesh_certificates" type="object">
      Configuration for issuance of mTLS keys and certificates to Kubernetes pods. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="enable_certificates" type="bool">
          enable_certificates controls issuance of workload mTLS certificates. If set, the GKE Workload Identity Certificates controller and node agent will be deployed in the cluster, which can then be configured by creating a WorkloadCertificateConfig Custom Resource. Requires Workload Identity (workload_pool must be non-empty). Optional.
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="monitoring_config" type="object">
      MonitoringConfig is cluster monitoring configuration. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="advanced_datapath_observability_config" type="object">
          AdvancedDatapathObservabilityConfig specifies configuration of observability features of advanced datapath. Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="enable_metrics" type="bool">
              Expose flow metrics on nodes Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="enable_relay" type="bool">
              Enable Relay component Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="relay_mode" type="string">
              Method used to make Relay available Optional.
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="component_config" type="object">
          MonitoringComponentConfig is cluster monitoring component configuration. Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="enable_components" type="list(string)">
              Select components to collect metrics. An empty set would disable all monitoring. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="managed_prometheus_config" type="object">
          ManagedPrometheusConfig defines the configuration for Google Cloud Managed Service for Prometheus. Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="auto_monitoring_config" type="object">
              AutoMonitoringConfig defines the configuration for GKE Workload Auto-Monitoring. Optional.
              <Expandable title="properties">
                <ResponseField name="scope" type="string">
                  Scope for GKE Workload Auto-Monitoring. Optional.
                </ResponseField>
              </Expandable>
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="enabled" type="bool">
              Enable Managed Collection. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="monitoring_service" type="string">
      The monitoring service the cluster should use to write metrics. Currently available options: * `monitoring.googleapis.com/kubernetes` - The Cloud Monitoring service with a Kubernetes-native resource model * `monitoring.googleapis.com` - The legacy Cloud Monitoring service (no longer available as of GKE 1.15). * `none` - No metrics will be exported from the cluster. If left as an empty string,`monitoring.googleapis.com/kubernetes` will be used for GKE 1.14+ or `monitoring.googleapis.com` for earlier versions. Optional.
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="name" type="string">
      The name of this cluster. The name must be unique within this project and location (e.g. zone or region), and can be up to 40 characters with the following restrictions: * Lowercase letters, numbers, and hyphens only. * Must start with a letter. * Must end with a number or a letter. Optional.
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="network" type="string">
      The name of the Google Compute Engine [network](https://cloud.google.com/compute/docs/networks-and-firewalls#networks) to which the cluster is connected. If left unspecified, the `default` network will be used. Optional.
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="network_config" type="object">
      NetworkConfig reports the relative names of network & subnetwork. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="datapath_provider" type="string">
          The desired datapath provider for this cluster. By default, uses the IPTables-based kube-proxy implementation. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="dataplane_v2_config" type="object">
          DataplaneV2Config is the configuration for DPv2. Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="scalability_mode" type="string">
              Optional. Scalability mode for the cluster. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="default_enable_private_nodes" type="bool">
          Controls whether by default nodes have private IP addresses only. It is invalid to specify both PrivateClusterConfig.enablePrivateNodes and this field at the same time. To update the default setting, use ClusterUpdate.desired_default_enable_private_nodes Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="default_snat_status" type="object">
          DefaultSnatStatus contains the desired state of whether default sNAT should be disabled on the cluster. Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="disabled" type="bool">
              Disables cluster default sNAT rules. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="disable_l4_lb_firewall_reconciliation" type="bool">
          Disable L4 load balancer VPC firewalls to enable firewall policies. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="dns_config" type="object">
          DNSConfig contains the desired set of options for configuring clusterDNS. Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="additive_vpc_scope_dns_domain" type="string">
              Optional. The domain used in Additive VPC scope. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="cluster_dns" type="string">
              cluster_dns indicates which in-cluster DNS provider should be used. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="cluster_dns_domain" type="string">
              cluster_dns_domain is the suffix used for all cluster service records. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="cluster_dns_scope" type="string">
              cluster_dns_scope indicates the scope of access to cluster DNS records. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="enable_cilium_clusterwide_network_policy" type="bool">
          Whether CiliumClusterwideNetworkPolicy is enabled on this cluster. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="enable_fqdn_network_policy" type="bool">
          Whether FQDN Network Policy is enabled on this cluster. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="enable_intra_node_visibility" type="bool">
          Whether Intra-node visibility is enabled for this cluster. This makes same node pod to pod traffic visible for VPC network. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="enable_l4ilb_subsetting" type="bool">
          Whether L4ILB Subsetting is enabled for this cluster. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="enable_multi_networking" type="bool">
          Whether multi-networking is enabled for this cluster. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="gateway_api_config" type="object">
          GatewayAPIConfig contains the desired config of Gateway API on this cluster. Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="channel" type="string">
              The Gateway API release channel to use for Gateway API. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="in_transit_encryption_config" type="string">
          Specify the details of in-transit encryption. Now named inter-node transparent encryption. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="network" type="string">
          Output only. The relative name of the Google Compute Engine [network](https://cloud.google.com/compute/docs/networks-and-firewalls#networks) to which the cluster is connected. Example: projects/my-project/global/networks/my-network Computed by Google Cloud after `ship`.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="network_performance_config" type="object">
          Configuration of network bandwidth tiers Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="total_egress_bandwidth_tier" type="string">
              Specifies the total network bandwidth tier for NodePools in the cluster. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="private_ipv6_google_access" type="string">
          The desired state of IPv6 connectivity to Google Services. By default, no private IPv6 access to or from Google Services (all access will be via IPv4) Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="service_external_ips_config" type="object">
          Config to block services with externalIPs field. Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="enabled" type="bool">
              Whether Services with ExternalIPs field are allowed or not. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="subnetwork" type="string">
          Output only. The relative name of the Google Compute Engine [subnetwork](https://cloud.google.com/compute/docs/vpc) to which the cluster is connected. Example: projects/my-project/regions/us-central1/subnetworks/my-subnet Computed by Google Cloud after `ship`.
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="network_policy" type="object">
      Configuration options for the NetworkPolicy feature. https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/services-networking/networkpolicies/ Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="enabled" type="bool">
          Whether network policy is enabled on the cluster. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="provider" type="string">
          The selected network policy provider. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="node_config" type="object">
      Parameters that describe the nodes in a cluster. GKE Autopilot clusters do not recognize parameters in `NodeConfig`. Use AutoprovisioningNodePoolDefaults instead. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="accelerators" type="list(object)">
          A list of hardware accelerators to be attached to each node. See https://cloud.google.com/compute/docs/gpus for more information about support for GPUs. Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="accelerator_count" type="string">
              How many accelerator (GPU/TPU) devices are attached per node. Optional; if omitted, computed by Google Cloud. **(AI-inferred)**
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="accelerator_type" type="string">
              The accelerator (GPU/TPU) type attached to each node, e.g. `nvidia-tesla-t4`. Optional; if omitted, computed by Google Cloud. **(AI-inferred)**
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="gpu_driver_installation_config" type="object">
              Whether and how GKE automatically installs the GPU driver on nodes with attached accelerators. Optional; if omitted, computed by Google Cloud. **(AI-inferred)**
              <Expandable title="properties">
                <ResponseField name="gpu_driver_version" type="string">
                  Which GPU driver version GKE installs, e.g. `LATEST` or `DEFAULT`. Optional; if omitted, computed by Google Cloud. **(AI-inferred)**
                </ResponseField>
              </Expandable>
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="gpu_partition_size" type="string">
              The fraction of a physical GPU allocated per partition, when GPU partitioning (e.g. MIG) is used instead of a whole device. Optional; if omitted, computed by Google Cloud. **(AI-inferred)**
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="gpu_sharing_config" type="object">
              Configuration letting multiple containers share a single physical GPU, rather than each requiring a dedicated one. Optional; if omitted, computed by Google Cloud. **(AI-inferred)**
              <Expandable title="properties">
                <ResponseField name="gpu_sharing_strategy" type="string">
                  How a shared GPU is divided among containers, e.g. `TIME_SHARING` or `MPS`. Optional; if omitted, computed by Google Cloud. **(AI-inferred)**
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="max_shared_clients_per_gpu" type="string">
                  The maximum number of containers that may share a single physical GPU. Optional; if omitted, computed by Google Cloud. **(AI-inferred)**
                </ResponseField>
              </Expandable>
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="advanced_machine_features" type="object">
          Specifies options for controlling advanced machine features. Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="enable_nested_virtualization" type="bool">
              Whether or not to enable nested virtualization (defaults to false). Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="performance_monitoring_unit" type="string">
              Type of Performance Monitoring Unit (PMU) requested on node pool instances. If unset, PMU will not be available to the node. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="threads_per_core" type="string">
              The number of threads per physical core. To disable simultaneous multithreading (SMT) set this to 1. If unset, the maximum number of threads supported per core by the underlying processor is assumed. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="boot_disk" type="object">
          BootDisk specifies the boot disk configuration for node pools. Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="disk_type" type="string">
              Disk type of the boot disk. (i.e. Hyperdisk-Balanced, PD-Balanced, etc.) Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="provisioned_iops" type="string">
              For Hyperdisk-Balanced only, the provisioned IOPS config value. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="provisioned_throughput" type="string">
              For Hyperdisk-Balanced only, the provisioned throughput config value. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="size_gb" type="string">
              Disk size in GB. Replaces NodeConfig.disk_size_gb Optional.
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="boot_disk_kms_key" type="string">
          The Customer Managed Encryption Key used to encrypt the boot disk attached to each node in the node pool. This should be of the form projects/[KEY_PROJECT_ID]/locations/[LOCATION]/keyRings/[RING_NAME]/cryptoKeys/[KEY_NAME]. For more information about protecting resources with Cloud KMS Keys please see: https://cloud.google.com/compute/docs/disks/customer-managed-encryption Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="confidential_nodes" type="object">
          ConfidentialNodes is configuration for the confidential nodes feature, which makes nodes run on confidential VMs. Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="confidential_instance_type" type="string">
              Defines the type of technology used by the confidential node. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="enabled" type="bool">
              Whether Confidential Nodes feature is enabled. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="consolidation_delay" type="string">
          Consolidation delay defines duration after which the Cluster Autoscaler can scale down underutilized nodes. If not set, nodes are scaled down by default behavior, i.e. according to the chosen autoscaling profile. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="containerd_config" type="object">
          ContainerdConfig contains configuration to customize containerd. Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="private_registry_access_config" type="object">
              PrivateRegistryAccessConfig contains access configuration for private container registries. Optional.
              <Expandable title="properties">
                <ResponseField name="certificate_authority_domain_config" type="list(object)">
                  Private registry access configuration. Optional.
                  <Expandable title="properties">
                    <ResponseField name="fqdns" type="list(string)">
                      The fully qualified domain name(s) of the private registry/registries this certificate is trusted for. Optional; if omitted, computed by Google Cloud. **(AI-inferred)**
                    </ResponseField>
                    <ResponseField name="gcp_secret_manager_certificate_config" type="object">
                      A CA certificate sourced from Secret Manager, trusted for verifying a private container registry's own TLS certificate. Optional; if omitted, computed by Google Cloud. **(AI-inferred)**
                      <Expandable title="properties">
