                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="hugepage_size2m" type="number">
                  Optional. Amount of 2M hugepages Optional.
                </ResponseField>
              </Expandable>
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="node_kernel_module_loading" type="object">
              Configuration for kernel module loading on nodes. Optional.
              <Expandable title="properties">
                <ResponseField name="policy" type="string">
                  Set the node module loading policy for nodes in the node pool. Optional.
                </ResponseField>
              </Expandable>
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="node_vfio_config" type="object">
              Configuration settings for VFIO (Virtual Function I/O) on a node. VFIO allows safe, unprivileged, userspace drivers to access I/O devices. Optional.
              <Expandable title="properties">
                <ResponseField name="dma_entry_limit" type="number">
                  Optional. Specifies the maximum number of DMA entries (pages) that can be mapped by the VFIO IOMMU type 1 driver for a container. This limit affects the total amount of host memory that can be pinned for direct device access, which is often critical for high-performance devices like TPUs and GPUs. This setting corresponds to the kernel parameter at: `/sys/module/vfio_iommu_type1/parameters/dma_entry_limit`. The default value in the kernel is `65535`. Higher values may be needed for workloads mapping large memory regions. Supported values are integers between `65535` and `4194304`. Optional.
                </ResponseField>
              </Expandable>
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="swap_config" type="object">
              Configuration for swap memory on a node pool. Optional.
              <Expandable title="properties">
                <ResponseField name="boot_disk_profile" type="object">
                  Swap on the node's boot disk. Optional.
                  <Expandable title="properties">
                    <ResponseField name="swap_size_gib" type="string">
                      Specifies the size of the swap space in gibibytes (GiB). Optional.
                    </ResponseField>
                    <ResponseField name="swap_size_percent" type="number">
                      Specifies the size of the swap space as a percentage of the boot disk size. Optional.
                    </ResponseField>
                  </Expandable>
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="dedicated_local_ssd_profile" type="object">
                  Provisions a new, separate local NVMe SSD exclusively for swap. Optional.
                  <Expandable title="properties">
                    <ResponseField name="disk_count" type="string">
                      The number of physical local NVMe SSD disks to attach. Optional.
                    </ResponseField>
                  </Expandable>
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="enabled" type="bool">
                  Optional. Enables or disables swap for the node pool. Optional.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="encryption_config" type="object">
                  Defines encryption settings for the swap space. Optional.
                  <Expandable title="properties">
                    <ResponseField name="disabled" type="bool">
                      Optional. If true, swap space will not be encrypted. Defaults to false (encrypted). Optional.
                    </ResponseField>
                  </Expandable>
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="ephemeral_local_ssd_profile" type="object">
                  Swap on the local SSD shared with pod ephemeral storage. Optional.
                  <Expandable title="properties">
                    <ResponseField name="swap_size_gib" type="string">
                      Specifies the size of the swap space in gibibytes (GiB). Optional.
                    </ResponseField>
                    <ResponseField name="swap_size_percent" type="number">
                      Specifies the size of the swap space as a percentage of the ephemeral local SSD capacity. Optional.
                    </ResponseField>
                  </Expandable>
                </ResponseField>
              </Expandable>
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="sysctls" type="map(string)">
              The Linux kernel parameters to be applied to the nodes and all pods running on the nodes. The following parameters are supported. net.core.busy_poll net.core.busy_read net.core.netdev_max_backlog net.core.rmem_max net.core.rmem_default net.core.wmem_default net.core.wmem_max net.core.optmem_max net.core.somaxconn net.ipv4.neigh.default.gc_thresh1 net.ipv4.neigh.default.gc_thresh2 net.ipv4.neigh.default.gc_thresh3 net.ipv4.tcp_rmem net.ipv4.tcp_wmem net.ipv4.tcp_tw_reuse net.ipv4.tcp_mtu_probing net.ipv4.tcp_max_orphans net.ipv4.tcp_max_tw_buckets net.ipv4.tcp_syn_retries net.ipv4.tcp_ecn net.ipv4.tcp_congestion_control net.netfilter.nf_conntrack_max net.netfilter.nf_conntrack_buckets net.netfilter.nf_conntrack_tcp_timeout_close_wait net.netfilter.nf_conntrack_tcp_timeout_time_wait net.netfilter.nf_conntrack_tcp_timeout_established net.netfilter.nf_conntrack_acct kernel.keys.maxkeys kernel.keys.maxbytes kernel.shmmni kernel.shmmax kernel.shmall kernel.core_pattern kernel.perf_event_paranoid kernel.sched_rt_runtime_us kernel.softlockup_panic kernel.yama.ptrace_scope kernel.kptr_restrict kernel.dmesg_restrict kernel.sysrq fs.aio-max-nr fs.file-max fs.inotify.max_user_instances fs.inotify.max_user_watches fs.nr_open vm.dirty_background_ratio vm.dirty_background_bytes vm.dirty_expire_centisecs vm.dirty_ratio vm.dirty_bytes vm.dirty_writeback_centisecs vm.max_map_count vm.overcommit_memory vm.overcommit_ratio vm.vfs_cache_pressure vm.swappiness vm.watermark_scale_factor vm.min_free_kbytes Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="transparent_hugepage_defrag" type="string">
              Optional. Defines the transparent hugepage defrag configuration on the node. VM hugepage allocation can be managed by either limiting defragmentation for delayed allocation or skipping it entirely for immediate allocation only. See https://docs.kernel.org/admin-guide/mm/transhuge.html for more details. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="transparent_hugepage_enabled" type="string">
              Optional. Transparent hugepage support for anonymous memory can be entirely disabled (mostly for debugging purposes) or only enabled inside MADV_HUGEPAGE regions (to avoid the risk of consuming more memory resources) or enabled system wide. See https://docs.kernel.org/admin-guide/mm/transhuge.html for more details. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="network_tags" type="object">
          Collection of Compute Engine network tags that can be applied to a node's underlying VM instance. Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="tags" type="list(string)">
              List of network tags. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="node_kubelet_config" type="object">
          Node kubelet configs. Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="allowed_unsafe_sysctls" type="list(string)">
              Optional. Defines a comma-separated allowlist of unsafe sysctls or sysctl patterns (ending in `*`). The unsafe namespaced sysctl groups are `kernel.shm*`, `kernel.msg*`, `kernel.sem`, `fs.mqueue.*`, and `net.*`. Leaving this allowlist empty means they cannot be set on Pods. To allow certain sysctls or sysctl patterns to be set on Pods, list them separated by commas. For example: `kernel.msg*,net.ipv4.route.min_pmtu`. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/tasks/administer-cluster/sysctl-cluster/ for more details. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="container_log_max_files" type="number">
              Optional. Defines the maximum number of container log files that can be present for a container. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/cluster-administration/logging/#log-rotation The value must be an integer between 2 and 10, inclusive. The default value is 5 if unspecified. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="container_log_max_size" type="string">
              Optional. Defines the maximum size of the container log file before it is rotated. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/cluster-administration/logging/#log-rotation Valid format is positive number + unit, e.g. 100Ki, 10Mi. Valid units are Ki, Mi, Gi. The value must be between 10Mi and 500Mi, inclusive. Note that the total container log size (container_log_max_size * container_log_max_files) cannot exceed 1% of the total storage of the node, to avoid disk pressure caused by log files. The default value is 10Mi if unspecified. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="cpu_cfs_quota" type="bool">
              Enable CPU CFS quota enforcement for containers that specify CPU limits. This option is enabled by default which makes kubelet use CFS quota (https://www.kernel.org/doc/Documentation/scheduler/sched-bwc.txt) to enforce container CPU limits. Otherwise, CPU limits will not be enforced at all. Disable this option to mitigate CPU throttling problems while still having your pods to be in Guaranteed QoS class by specifying the CPU limits. The default value is 'true' if unspecified. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="cpu_cfs_quota_period" type="string">
              Set the CPU CFS quota period value 'cpu.cfs_period_us'. The string must be a sequence of decimal numbers, each with optional fraction and a unit suffix, such as "300ms". Valid time units are "ns", "us" (or "µs"), "ms", "s", "m", "h". The value must be a positive duration between 1ms and 1 second, inclusive. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="cpu_manager_policy" type="string">
              Control the CPU management policy on the node. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/tasks/administer-cluster/cpu-management-policies/ The following values are allowed. * "none": the default, which represents the existing scheduling behavior. * "static": allows pods with certain resource characteristics to be granted increased CPU affinity and exclusivity on the node. The default value is 'none' if unspecified. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="crash_loop_back_off" type="object">
              Contains config to modify node-level parameters for container restart behavior. Optional.
              <Expandable title="properties">
                <ResponseField name="max_container_restart_period" type="string">
                  Optional. The maximum duration the backoff delay can accrue to for container restarts, minimum 1 second, maximum 300 seconds. If not set, defaults to the internal crashloopbackoff maximum. The string must be a sequence of decimal numbers, each with optional fraction and a unit suffix, such as "300ms". Valid time units are "ns", "us" (or "µs"), "ms", "s", "m", "h". See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/workloads/pods/pod-lifecycle/#configurable-container-restart-delay for more details. Optional.
                </ResponseField>
              </Expandable>
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="eviction_max_pod_grace_period_seconds" type="number">
              Optional. eviction_max_pod_grace_period_seconds is the maximum allowed grace period (in seconds) to use when terminating pods in response to a soft eviction threshold being met. This value effectively caps the Pod's terminationGracePeriodSeconds value during soft evictions. Default: 0. Range: [0, 300]. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="eviction_minimum_reclaim" type="object">
              Eviction minimum reclaims are the resource amounts of minimum reclaims for each eviction signal. Optional.
              <Expandable title="properties">
                <ResponseField name="imagefs_available" type="string">
                  Optional. Minimum reclaim for eviction due to imagefs available signal. Only take percentage value for now. Sample format: "10%". Must be &lt;=10%. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/node-pressure-eviction/#eviction-signals Optional.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="imagefs_inodes_free" type="string">
                  Optional. Minimum reclaim for eviction due to imagefs inodes free signal. Only take percentage value for now. Sample format: "10%". Must be &lt;=10%. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/node-pressure-eviction/#eviction-signals Optional.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="memory_available" type="string">
                  Optional. Minimum reclaim for eviction due to memory available signal. Only take percentage value for now. Sample format: "10%". Must be &lt;=10%. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/node-pressure-eviction/#eviction-signals Optional.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="nodefs_available" type="string">
                  Optional. Minimum reclaim for eviction due to nodefs available signal. Only take percentage value for now. Sample format: "10%". Must be &lt;=10%. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/node-pressure-eviction/#eviction-signals Optional.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="nodefs_inodes_free" type="string">
                  Optional. Minimum reclaim for eviction due to nodefs inodes free signal. Only take percentage value for now. Sample format: "10%". Must be &lt;=10%. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/node-pressure-eviction/#eviction-signals Optional.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="pid_available" type="string">
                  Optional. Minimum reclaim for eviction due to pid available signal. Only take percentage value for now. Sample format: "10%". Must be &lt;=10%. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/node-pressure-eviction/#eviction-signals Optional.
                </ResponseField>
              </Expandable>
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="eviction_soft" type="object">
              Eviction signals are the current state of a particular resource at a specific point in time. The kubelet uses eviction signals to make eviction decisions by comparing the signals to eviction thresholds, which are the minimum amount of the resource that should be available on the node. Optional.
              <Expandable title="properties">
                <ResponseField name="imagefs_available" type="string">
                  Optional. Amount of storage available on filesystem that container runtime uses for storing images layers. If the container filesystem and image filesystem are not separate, then imagefs can store both image layers and writeable layers. Defines the amount of "imagefs.available" signal in kubelet. Default is unset, if not specified in the kubelet config. It takses percentage value for now. Sample format: "30%". Must be &gt;= 15% and &lt;= 50%. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/node-pressure-eviction/#eviction-signals Optional.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="imagefs_inodes_free" type="string">
                  Optional. Amount of inodes available on filesystem that container runtime uses for storing images layers. Defines the amount of "imagefs.inodesFree" signal in kubelet. Default is unset, if not specified in the kubelet config. Linux only. It takses percentage value for now. Sample format: "30%". Must be &gt;= 5% and &lt;= 50%. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/node-pressure-eviction/#eviction-signals Optional.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="memory_available" type="string">
                  Optional. Memory available (i.e. capacity - workingSet), in bytes. Defines the amount of "memory.available" signal in kubelet. Default is unset, if not specified in the kubelet config. Format: positive number + unit, e.g. 100Ki, 10Mi, 5Gi. Valid units are Ki, Mi, Gi. Must be &gt;= 100Mi and &lt;= 50% of the node's memory. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/node-pressure-eviction/#eviction-signals Optional.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="nodefs_available" type="string">
                  Optional. Amount of storage available on filesystem that kubelet uses for volumes, daemon logs, etc. Defines the amount of "nodefs.available" signal in kubelet. Default is unset, if not specified in the kubelet config. It takses percentage value for now. Sample format: "30%". Must be &gt;= 10% and &lt;= 50%. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/node-pressure-eviction/#eviction-signals Optional.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="nodefs_inodes_free" type="string">
                  Optional. Amount of inodes available on filesystem that kubelet uses for volumes, daemon logs, etc. Defines the amount of "nodefs.inodesFree" signal in kubelet. Default is unset, if not specified in the kubelet config. Linux only. It takses percentage value for now. Sample format: "30%". Must be &gt;= 5% and &lt;= 50%. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/node-pressure-eviction/#eviction-signals Optional.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="pid_available" type="string">
                  Optional. Amount of PID available for pod allocation. Defines the amount of "pid.available" signal in kubelet. Default is unset, if not specified in the kubelet config. It takses percentage value for now. Sample format: "30%". Must be &gt;= 10% and &lt;= 50%. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/node-pressure-eviction/#eviction-signals Optional.
                </ResponseField>
              </Expandable>
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="eviction_soft_grace_period" type="object">
              Eviction grace periods are grace periods for each eviction signal. Optional.
              <Expandable title="properties">
                <ResponseField name="imagefs_available" type="string">
                  Optional. Grace period for eviction due to imagefs available signal. Sample format: "10s". Must be &gt;= 0. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/node-pressure-eviction/#eviction-signals Optional.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="imagefs_inodes_free" type="string">
                  Optional. Grace period for eviction due to imagefs inodes free signal. Sample format: "10s". Must be &gt;= 0. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/node-pressure-eviction/#eviction-signals Optional.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="memory_available" type="string">
                  Optional. Grace period for eviction due to memory available signal. Sample format: "10s". Must be &gt;= 0. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/node-pressure-eviction/#eviction-signals Optional.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="nodefs_available" type="string">
                  Optional. Grace period for eviction due to nodefs available signal. Sample format: "10s". Must be &gt;= 0. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/node-pressure-eviction/#eviction-signals Optional.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="nodefs_inodes_free" type="string">
                  Optional. Grace period for eviction due to nodefs inodes free signal. Sample format: "10s". Must be &gt;= 0. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/node-pressure-eviction/#eviction-signals Optional.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="pid_available" type="string">
                  Optional. Grace period for eviction due to pid available signal. Sample format: "10s". Must be &gt;= 0. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/node-pressure-eviction/#eviction-signals Optional.
                </ResponseField>
              </Expandable>
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="image_gc_high_threshold_percent" type="number">
              Optional. Defines the percent of disk usage after which image garbage collection is always run. The percent is calculated as this field value out of 100. The value must be between 10 and 85, inclusive and greater than image_gc_low_threshold_percent. The default value is 85 if unspecified. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="image_gc_low_threshold_percent" type="number">
              Optional. Defines the percent of disk usage before which image garbage collection is never run. Lowest disk usage to garbage collect to. The percent is calculated as this field value out of 100. The value must be between 10 and 85, inclusive and smaller than image_gc_high_threshold_percent. The default value is 80 if unspecified. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="image_maximum_gc_age" type="string">
              Optional. Defines the maximum age an image can be unused before it is garbage collected. The string must be a sequence of decimal numbers, each with optional fraction and a unit suffix, such as "300s", "1.5h", and "2h45m". Valid time units are "ns", "us" (or "µs"), "ms", "s", "m", "h". The value must be a positive duration greater than image_minimum_gc_age or "0s". The default value is "0s" if unspecified, which disables this field, meaning images won't be garbage collected based on being unused for too long. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="image_minimum_gc_age" type="string">
              Optional. Defines the minimum age for an unused image before it is garbage collected. The string must be a sequence of decimal numbers, each with optional fraction and a unit suffix, such as "300s", "1.5h", and "2h45m". Valid time units are "ns", "us" (or "µs"), "ms", "s", "m", "h". The value must be a positive duration less than or equal to 2 minutes. The default value is "2m0s" if unspecified. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="insecure_kubelet_readonly_port_enabled" type="bool">
              Enable or disable Kubelet read only port. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="max_parallel_image_pulls" type="number">
              Optional. Defines the maximum number of image pulls in parallel. The range is 2 to 5, inclusive. The default value is 2 or 3 depending on the disk type. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/containers/images/#maximum-parallel-image-pulls for more details. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="memory_manager" type="object">
              The option enables the Kubernetes NUMA-aware Memory Manager feature. Detailed description about the feature can be found [here](https://kubernetes.io/docs/tasks/administer-cluster/memory-manager/). Optional.
              <Expandable title="properties">
                <ResponseField name="policy" type="string">
                  Controls the memory management policy on the Node. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/tasks/administer-cluster/memory-manager/#policies The following values are allowed. * "none" * "static" The default value is 'none' if unspecified. Optional.
                </ResponseField>
              </Expandable>
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="pod_pids_limit" type="string">
              Set the Pod PID limits. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/policy/pid-limiting/#pod-pid-limits Controls the maximum number of processes allowed to run in a pod. The value must be greater than or equal to 1024 and less than 4194304. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="shutdown_grace_period_critical_pods_seconds" type="number">
              Optional. shutdown_grace_period_critical_pods_seconds is the maximum allowed grace period (in seconds) used to terminate critical pods during a node shutdown. This value should be &lt;= shutdown_grace_period_seconds, and is only valid if shutdown_grace_period_seconds is set. https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/cluster-administration/node-shutdown/ Range: [0, 120]. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="shutdown_grace_period_seconds" type="number">
              Optional. shutdown_grace_period_seconds is the maximum allowed grace period (in seconds) the total duration that the node should delay the shutdown during a graceful shutdown. This is the total grace period for pod termination for both regular and critical pods. https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/cluster-administration/node-shutdown/ If set to 0, node will not enable the graceful node shutdown functionality. This field is only valid for Spot VMs. Allowed values: 0, 30, 120. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="single_process_oom_kill" type="bool">
              Optional. Defines whether to enable single process OOM killer. If true, will prevent the memory.oom.group flag from being set for container cgroups in cgroups v2. This causes processes in the container to be OOM killed individually instead of as a group. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="topology_manager" type="object">
              TopologyManager defines the configuration options for the [`kubelet` Topology Manager component](https://kubernetes.io/docs/tasks/administer-cluster/topology-manager/). For more information about the supported machine types and versions for the Topology Manager in GKE, see [Customizing node system configuration](https://docs.cloud.google.com/kubernetes-engine/docs/how-to/node-system-config#kubelet-resource-managers). Optional.
              <Expandable title="properties">
                <ResponseField name="policy" type="string">
                  Configures the strategy for resource alignment. Allowed values are: * none: the default policy, and does not perform any topology alignment. * restricted: the topology manager stores the preferred NUMA node affinity for the container, and will reject the pod if the affinity if not preferred. * best-effort: the topology manager stores the preferred NUMA node affinity for the container. If the affinity is not preferred, the topology manager will admit the pod to the node anyway. * single-numa-node: the topology manager determines if the single NUMA node affinity is possible. If it is, Topology Manager will store this and the Hint Providers can then use this information when making the resource allocation decision. If, however, this is not possible then the Topology Manager will reject the pod from the node. This will result in a pod in a Terminated state with a pod admission failure. The default policy value is 'none' if unspecified. Details about each strategy can be found [here](https://kubernetes.io/docs/tasks/administer-cluster/topology-manager/#topology-manager-policies). Optional.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="scope" type="string">
                  The Topology Manager aligns resources in following scopes: * container * pod The default scope is 'container' if unspecified. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/tasks/administer-cluster/topology-manager/#topology-manager-scopes Optional.
                </ResponseField>
              </Expandable>
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="resource_manager_tags" type="object">
          A map of resource manager tag keys and values to be attached to the nodes for managing Compute Engine firewalls using Network Firewall Policies. Tags must be according to specifications in https://cloud.google.com/vpc/docs/tags-firewalls-overview#specifications. A maximum of 5 tag key-value pairs can be specified. Existing tags will be replaced with new values. Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="tags" type="map(string)">
              TagKeyValue must be in one of the following formats ([KEY]=[VALUE]) 1. `tagKeys/&#123;tag_key_id&#125;=tagValues/&#123;tag_value_id&#125;` 2. `&#123;org_id&#125;/&#123;tag_key_name&#125;=&#123;tag_value_name&#125;` 3. `&#123;project_id&#125;/&#123;tag_key_name&#125;=&#123;tag_value_name&#125;` Optional.
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="node_pool_defaults" type="object">
      Subset of Nodepool message that has defaults. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="node_config_defaults" type="object">
          Subset of NodeConfig message that has defaults. Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="containerd_config" type="object">
              ContainerdConfig contains configuration to customize containerd. Optional.
              <Expandable title="properties">
                <ResponseField name="private_registry_access_config" type="object">
                  PrivateRegistryAccessConfig contains access configuration for private container registries. Optional.
                  <Expandable title="properties">
                    <ResponseField name="certificate_authority_domain_config" type="list(object)">
                      Private registry access configuration. Optional.
                      <Expandable title="properties">
                        <ResponseField name="fqdns" type="list(string)">
                          The fully qualified domain name(s) of the private registry/registries this certificate is trusted for. Optional; if omitted, computed by Google Cloud. **(AI-inferred)**
                        </ResponseField>
                        <ResponseField name="gcp_secret_manager_certificate_config" type="object">
                          A CA certificate sourced from Secret Manager, trusted for verifying a private container registry's own TLS certificate. Optional; if omitted, computed by Google Cloud. **(AI-inferred)**
                          <Expandable title="properties">
                            <ResponseField name="secret_uri" type="string">
                              The Secret Manager secret version this value is sourced from. Optional; if omitted, computed by Google Cloud. **(AI-inferred)**
                            </ResponseField>
                          </Expandable>
                        </ResponseField>
                      </Expandable>
                    </ResponseField>
                    <ResponseField name="enabled" type="bool">
                      Private registry access is enabled. Optional.
                    </ResponseField>
                  </Expandable>
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="registry_hosts" type="list(object)">
                  RegistryHostConfig configures containerd registry host configuration. Each registry_hosts represents a hosts.toml file. At most 25 registry_hosts are allowed. Optional.
                  <Expandable title="properties">
                    <ResponseField name="hosts" type="list(object)">
                      The host(s) this configuration applies to. Optional; if omitted, computed by Google Cloud. **(AI-inferred)**
                      <Expandable title="properties">
                        <ResponseField name="ca" type="list(object)">
                          The CA certificate used to verify the remote server's own identity. Optional; if omitted, computed by Google Cloud. **(AI-inferred)**
                          <Expandable title="properties">
                            <ResponseField name="gcp_secret_manager_secret_uri" type="string">

            <ResponseField name="logging_config" type="object">
              NodePoolLoggingConfig specifies logging configuration for node pools. Optional.
              <Expandable title="properties">
                <ResponseField name="variant_config" type="object">
                  LoggingVariantConfig specifies the behaviour of the logging component. Optional.
                  <Expandable title="properties">

            <ResponseField name="node_kubelet_config" type="object">
              Node kubelet configs. Optional.
              <Expandable title="properties">
                <ResponseField name="allowed_unsafe_sysctls" type="list(string)">
                  Optional. Defines a comma-separated allowlist of unsafe sysctls or sysctl patterns (ending in `*`). The unsafe namespaced sysctl groups are `kernel.shm*`, `kernel.msg*`, `kernel.sem`, `fs.mqueue.*`, and `net.*`. Leaving this allowlist empty means they cannot be set on Pods. To allow certain sysctls or sysctl patterns to be set on Pods, list them separated by commas. For example: `kernel.msg*,net.ipv4.route.min_pmtu`. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/tasks/administer-cluster/sysctl-cluster/ for more details. Optional.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="container_log_max_files" type="number">
                  Optional. Defines the maximum number of container log files that can be present for a container. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/cluster-administration/logging/#log-rotation The value must be an integer between 2 and 10, inclusive. The default value is 5 if unspecified. Optional.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="container_log_max_size" type="string">
                  Optional. Defines the maximum size of the container log file before it is rotated. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/cluster-administration/logging/#log-rotation Valid format is positive number + unit, e.g. 100Ki, 10Mi. Valid units are Ki, Mi, Gi. The value must be between 10Mi and 500Mi, inclusive. Note that the total container log size (container_log_max_size * container_log_max_files) cannot exceed 1% of the total storage of the node, to avoid disk pressure caused by log files. The default value is 10Mi if unspecified. Optional.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="cpu_cfs_quota" type="bool">
                  Enable CPU CFS quota enforcement for containers that specify CPU limits. This option is enabled by default which makes kubelet use CFS quota (https://www.kernel.org/doc/Documentation/scheduler/sched-bwc.txt) to enforce container CPU limits. Otherwise, CPU limits will not be enforced at all. Disable this option to mitigate CPU throttling problems while still having your pods to be in Guaranteed QoS class by specifying the CPU limits. The default value is 'true' if unspecified. Optional.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="cpu_cfs_quota_period" type="string">
                  Set the CPU CFS quota period value 'cpu.cfs_period_us'. The string must be a sequence of decimal numbers, each with optional fraction and a unit suffix, such as "300ms". Valid time units are "ns", "us" (or "µs"), "ms", "s", "m", "h". The value must be a positive duration between 1ms and 1 second, inclusive. Optional.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="cpu_manager_policy" type="string">
                  Control the CPU management policy on the node. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/tasks/administer-cluster/cpu-management-policies/ The following values are allowed. * "none": the default, which represents the existing scheduling behavior. * "static": allows pods with certain resource characteristics to be granted increased CPU affinity and exclusivity on the node. The default value is 'none' if unspecified. Optional.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="crash_loop_back_off" type="object">
                  Contains config to modify node-level parameters for container restart behavior. Optional.
                  <Expandable title="properties">
                    <ResponseField name="max_container_restart_period" type="string">
                      Optional. The maximum duration the backoff delay can accrue to for container restarts, minimum 1 second, maximum 300 seconds. If not set, defaults to the internal crashloopbackoff maximum. The string must be a sequence of decimal numbers, each with optional fraction and a unit suffix, such as "300ms". Valid time units are "ns", "us" (or "µs"), "ms", "s", "m", "h". See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/workloads/pods/pod-lifecycle/#configurable-container-restart-delay for more details. Optional.
                    </ResponseField>
                  </Expandable>
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="eviction_max_pod_grace_period_seconds" type="number">
                  Optional. eviction_max_pod_grace_period_seconds is the maximum allowed grace period (in seconds) to use when terminating pods in response to a soft eviction threshold being met. This value effectively caps the Pod's terminationGracePeriodSeconds value during soft evictions. Default: 0. Range: [0, 300]. Optional.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="eviction_minimum_reclaim" type="object">
                  Eviction minimum reclaims are the resource amounts of minimum reclaims for each eviction signal. Optional.
                  <Expandable title="properties">
                    <ResponseField name="imagefs_available" type="string">
                      Optional. Minimum reclaim for eviction due to imagefs available signal. Only take percentage value for now. Sample format: "10%". Must be &lt;=10%. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/node-pressure-eviction/#eviction-signals Optional.
                    </ResponseField>
                    <ResponseField name="imagefs_inodes_free" type="string">
                      Optional. Minimum reclaim for eviction due to imagefs inodes free signal. Only take percentage value for now. Sample format: "10%". Must be &lt;=10%. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/node-pressure-eviction/#eviction-signals Optional.
                    </ResponseField>
                    <ResponseField name="memory_available" type="string">
                      Optional. Minimum reclaim for eviction due to memory available signal. Only take percentage value for now. Sample format: "10%". Must be &lt;=10%. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/node-pressure-eviction/#eviction-signals Optional.
                    </ResponseField>
                    <ResponseField name="nodefs_available" type="string">
                      Optional. Minimum reclaim for eviction due to nodefs available signal. Only take percentage value for now. Sample format: "10%". Must be &lt;=10%. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/node-pressure-eviction/#eviction-signals Optional.
                    </ResponseField>
                    <ResponseField name="nodefs_inodes_free" type="string">
                      Optional. Minimum reclaim for eviction due to nodefs inodes free signal. Only take percentage value for now. Sample format: "10%". Must be &lt;=10%. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/node-pressure-eviction/#eviction-signals Optional.
                    </ResponseField>
                    <ResponseField name="pid_available" type="string">
                      Optional. Minimum reclaim for eviction due to pid available signal. Only take percentage value for now. Sample format: "10%". Must be &lt;=10%. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/node-pressure-eviction/#eviction-signals Optional.
                    </ResponseField>
                  </Expandable>
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="eviction_soft" type="object">
                  Eviction signals are the current state of a particular resource at a specific point in time. The kubelet uses eviction signals to make eviction decisions by comparing the signals to eviction thresholds, which are the minimum amount of the resource that should be available on the node. Optional.
                  <Expandable title="properties">
                    <ResponseField name="imagefs_available" type="string">
                      Optional. Amount of storage available on filesystem that container runtime uses for storing images layers. If the container filesystem and image filesystem are not separate, then imagefs can store both image layers and writeable layers. Defines the amount of "imagefs.available" signal in kubelet. Default is unset, if not specified in the kubelet config. It takses percentage value for now. Sample format: "30%". Must be &gt;= 15% and &lt;= 50%. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/node-pressure-eviction/#eviction-signals Optional.
                    </ResponseField>
                    <ResponseField name="imagefs_inodes_free" type="string">
                      Optional. Amount of inodes available on filesystem that container runtime uses for storing images layers. Defines the amount of "imagefs.inodesFree" signal in kubelet. Default is unset, if not specified in the kubelet config. Linux only. It takses percentage value for now. Sample format: "30%". Must be &gt;= 5% and &lt;= 50%. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/node-pressure-eviction/#eviction-signals Optional.
                    </ResponseField>
                    <ResponseField name="memory_available" type="string">
                      Optional. Memory available (i.e. capacity - workingSet), in bytes. Defines the amount of "memory.available" signal in kubelet. Default is unset, if not specified in the kubelet config. Format: positive number + unit, e.g. 100Ki, 10Mi, 5Gi. Valid units are Ki, Mi, Gi. Must be &gt;= 100Mi and &lt;= 50% of the node's memory. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/node-pressure-eviction/#eviction-signals Optional.
                    </ResponseField>
                    <ResponseField name="nodefs_available" type="string">
                      Optional. Amount of storage available on filesystem that kubelet uses for volumes, daemon logs, etc. Defines the amount of "nodefs.available" signal in kubelet. Default is unset, if not specified in the kubelet config. It takses percentage value for now. Sample format: "30%". Must be &gt;= 10% and &lt;= 50%. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/node-pressure-eviction/#eviction-signals Optional.
                    </ResponseField>
                    <ResponseField name="nodefs_inodes_free" type="string">
                      Optional. Amount of inodes available on filesystem that kubelet uses for volumes, daemon logs, etc. Defines the amount of "nodefs.inodesFree" signal in kubelet. Default is unset, if not specified in the kubelet config. Linux only. It takses percentage value for now. Sample format: "30%". Must be &gt;= 5% and &lt;= 50%. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/node-pressure-eviction/#eviction-signals Optional.
                    </ResponseField>
                    <ResponseField name="pid_available" type="string">
                      Optional. Amount of PID available for pod allocation. Defines the amount of "pid.available" signal in kubelet. Default is unset, if not specified in the kubelet config. It takses percentage value for now. Sample format: "30%". Must be &gt;= 10% and &lt;= 50%. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/node-pressure-eviction/#eviction-signals Optional.
                    </ResponseField>
                  </Expandable>
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="eviction_soft_grace_period" type="object">
                  Eviction grace periods are grace periods for each eviction signal. Optional.
                  <Expandable title="properties">
                    <ResponseField name="imagefs_available" type="string">
                      Optional. Grace period for eviction due to imagefs available signal. Sample format: "10s". Must be &gt;= 0. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/node-pressure-eviction/#eviction-signals Optional.
                    </ResponseField>
                    <ResponseField name="imagefs_inodes_free" type="string">
                      Optional. Grace period for eviction due to imagefs inodes free signal. Sample format: "10s". Must be &gt;= 0. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/node-pressure-eviction/#eviction-signals Optional.
                    </ResponseField>
                    <ResponseField name="memory_available" type="string">
                      Optional. Grace period for eviction due to memory available signal. Sample format: "10s". Must be &gt;= 0. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/node-pressure-eviction/#eviction-signals Optional.
                    </ResponseField>
                    <ResponseField name="nodefs_available" type="string">
                      Optional. Grace period for eviction due to nodefs available signal. Sample format: "10s". Must be &gt;= 0. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/node-pressure-eviction/#eviction-signals Optional.
                    </ResponseField>
                    <ResponseField name="nodefs_inodes_free" type="string">
                      Optional. Grace period for eviction due to nodefs inodes free signal. Sample format: "10s". Must be &gt;= 0. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/node-pressure-eviction/#eviction-signals Optional.
                    </ResponseField>
                    <ResponseField name="pid_available" type="string">
                      Optional. Grace period for eviction due to pid available signal. Sample format: "10s". Must be &gt;= 0. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/node-pressure-eviction/#eviction-signals Optional.
                    </ResponseField>
                  </Expandable>
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="image_gc_high_threshold_percent" type="number">
                  Optional. Defines the percent of disk usage after which image garbage collection is always run. The percent is calculated as this field value out of 100. The value must be between 10 and 85, inclusive and greater than image_gc_low_threshold_percent. The default value is 85 if unspecified. Optional.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="image_gc_low_threshold_percent" type="number">
                  Optional. Defines the percent of disk usage before which image garbage collection is never run. Lowest disk usage to garbage collect to. The percent is calculated as this field value out of 100. The value must be between 10 and 85, inclusive and smaller than image_gc_high_threshold_percent. The default value is 80 if unspecified. Optional.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="image_maximum_gc_age" type="string">
                  Optional. Defines the maximum age an image can be unused before it is garbage collected. The string must be a sequence of decimal numbers, each with optional fraction and a unit suffix, such as "300s", "1.5h", and "2h45m". Valid time units are "ns", "us" (or "µs"), "ms", "s", "m", "h". The value must be a positive duration greater than image_minimum_gc_age or "0s". The default value is "0s" if unspecified, which disables this field, meaning images won't be garbage collected based on being unused for too long. Optional.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="image_minimum_gc_age" type="string">
                  Optional. Defines the minimum age for an unused image before it is garbage collected. The string must be a sequence of decimal numbers, each with optional fraction and a unit suffix, such as "300s", "1.5h", and "2h45m". Valid time units are "ns", "us" (or "µs"), "ms", "s", "m", "h". The value must be a positive duration less than or equal to 2 minutes. The default value is "2m0s" if unspecified. Optional.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="insecure_kubelet_readonly_port_enabled" type="bool">
                  Enable or disable Kubelet read only port. Optional.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="max_parallel_image_pulls" type="number">
                  Optional. Defines the maximum number of image pulls in parallel. The range is 2 to 5, inclusive. The default value is 2 or 3 depending on the disk type. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/containers/images/#maximum-parallel-image-pulls for more details. Optional.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="memory_manager" type="object">
                  The option enables the Kubernetes NUMA-aware Memory Manager feature. Detailed description about the feature can be found [here](https://kubernetes.io/docs/tasks/administer-cluster/memory-manager/). Optional.
                  <Expandable title="properties">
                    <ResponseField name="policy" type="string">
                      Controls the memory management policy on the Node. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/tasks/administer-cluster/memory-manager/#policies The following values are allowed. * "none" * "static" The default value is 'none' if unspecified. Optional.
                    </ResponseField>
                  </Expandable>
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="pod_pids_limit" type="string">
                  Set the Pod PID limits. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/policy/pid-limiting/#pod-pid-limits Controls the maximum number of processes allowed to run in a pod. The value must be greater than or equal to 1024 and less than 4194304. Optional.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="shutdown_grace_period_critical_pods_seconds" type="number">
                  Optional. shutdown_grace_period_critical_pods_seconds is the maximum allowed grace period (in seconds) used to terminate critical pods during a node shutdown. This value should be &lt;= shutdown_grace_period_seconds, and is only valid if shutdown_grace_period_seconds is set. https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/cluster-administration/node-shutdown/ Range: [0, 120]. Optional.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="shutdown_grace_period_seconds" type="number">
                  Optional. shutdown_grace_period_seconds is the maximum allowed grace period (in seconds) the total duration that the node should delay the shutdown during a graceful shutdown. This is the total grace period for pod termination for both regular and critical pods. https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/cluster-administration/node-shutdown/ If set to 0, node will not enable the graceful node shutdown functionality. This field is only valid for Spot VMs. Allowed values: 0, 30, 120. Optional.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="single_process_oom_kill" type="bool">
                  Optional. Defines whether to enable single process OOM killer. If true, will prevent the memory.oom.group flag from being set for container cgroups in cgroups v2. This causes processes in the container to be OOM killed individually instead of as a group. Optional.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="topology_manager" type="object">
                  TopologyManager defines the configuration options for the [`kubelet` Topology Manager component](https://kubernetes.io/docs/tasks/administer-cluster/topology-manager/). For more information about the supported machine types and versions for the Topology Manager in GKE, see [Customizing node system configuration](https://docs.cloud.google.com/kubernetes-engine/docs/how-to/node-system-config#kubelet-resource-managers). Optional.
                  <Expandable title="properties">
                    <ResponseField name="policy" type="string">
                      Configures the strategy for resource alignment. Allowed values are: * none: the default policy, and does not perform any topology alignment. * restricted: the topology manager stores the preferred NUMA node affinity for the container, and will reject the pod if the affinity if not preferred. * best-effort: the topology manager stores the preferred NUMA node affinity for the container. If the affinity is not preferred, the topology manager will admit the pod to the node anyway. * single-numa-node: the topology manager determines if the single NUMA node affinity is possible. If it is, Topology Manager will store this and the Hint Providers can then use this information when making the resource allocation decision. If, however, this is not possible then the Topology Manager will reject the pod from the node. This will result in a pod in a Terminated state with a pod admission failure. The default policy value is 'none' if unspecified. Details about each strategy can be found [here](https://kubernetes.io/docs/tasks/administer-cluster/topology-manager/#topology-manager-policies). Optional.
                    </ResponseField>
                    <ResponseField name="scope" type="string">
                      The Topology Manager aligns resources in following scopes: * container * pod The default scope is 'container' if unspecified. See https://kubernetes.io/docs/tasks/administer-cluster/topology-manager/#topology-manager-scopes Optional.
                    </ResponseField>
                  </Expandable>
                </ResponseField>
              </Expandable>
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>

    <ResponseField name="notification_config" type="object">
      NotificationConfig is the configuration of notifications. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="pubsub" type="object">
          Pub/Sub specific notification config. Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="enabled" type="bool">
              Enable notifications for Pub/Sub. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="filter" type="object">
              Allows filtering to one or more specific event types. If event types are present, those and only those event types will be transmitted to the cluster. Other types will be skipped. If no filter is specified, or no event types are present, all event types will be sent Optional.
              <Expandable title="properties">
                <ResponseField name="event_type" type="list(string)">
                  Event types to allowlist. Optional.
                </ResponseField>
              </Expandable>
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="topic" type="string">
              The desired Pub/Sub topic to which notifications will be sent by GKE. Format is `projects/&#123;project&#125;/topics/&#123;topic&#125;`. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="parent_product_config" type="object">
      ParentProductConfig is the configuration of the parent product of the cluster. This field is used by Google internal products that are built on top of a GKE cluster and take the ownership of the cluster. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="labels" type="map(string)">
          Labels contain the configuration of the parent product. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="product_name" type="string">
          Name of the parent product associated with the cluster. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="pod_autoscaling" type="object">
      PodAutoscaling is used for configuration of parameters for workload autoscaling. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="hpa_profile" type="string">
          Selected Horizontal Pod Autoscaling profile. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="private_cluster_config" type="object">
      Configuration options for private clusters. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="enable_private_endpoint" type="bool">
          Whether the master's internal IP address is used as the cluster endpoint. Deprecated: Use ControlPlaneEndpointsConfig.IPEndpointsConfig.enable_public_endpoint instead. Note that the value of enable_public_endpoint is reversed: if enable_private_endpoint is false, then enable_public_endpoint will be true. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="enable_private_nodes" type="bool">
          Whether nodes have internal IP addresses only. If enabled, all nodes are given only RFC 1918 private addresses and communicate with the master via private networking. Deprecated: Use NetworkConfig.default_enable_private_nodes instead. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="master_global_access_config" type="object">
          Configuration for controlling master global access settings. Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="enabled" type="bool">
              Whenever master is accessible globally or not. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="master_ipv4_cidr_block" type="string">
          The IP range in CIDR notation to use for the hosted master network. This range will be used for assigning internal IP addresses to the master or set of masters, as well as the ILB VIP. This range must not overlap with any other ranges in use within the cluster's network. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="peering_name" type="string">
          Output only. The peering name in the customer VPC used by this cluster. Computed by Google Cloud after `ship`.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="private_endpoint" type="string">
          Output only. The internal IP address of this cluster's master endpoint. Deprecated: Use ControlPlaneEndpointsConfig.IPEndpointsConfig.private_endpoint instead. Computed by Google Cloud after `ship`.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="private_endpoint_subnetwork" type="string">
          Subnet to provision the master's private endpoint during cluster creation. Specified in projects/*/regions/*/subnetworks/* format. Deprecated: Use ControlPlaneEndpointsConfig.IPEndpointsConfig.private_endpoint_subnetwork instead. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="public_endpoint" type="string">
          Output only. The external IP address of this cluster's master endpoint. Deprecated:Use ControlPlaneEndpointsConfig.IPEndpointsConfig.public_endpoint instead. Computed by Google Cloud after `ship`.
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="rbac_binding_config" type="object">
      RBACBindingConfig allows user to restrict ClusterRoleBindings an RoleBindings that can be created. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="enable_insecure_binding_system_authenticated" type="bool">
          Setting this to true will allow any ClusterRoleBinding and RoleBinding with subjects system:authenticated. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="enable_insecure_binding_system_unauthenticated" type="bool">
          Setting this to true will allow any ClusterRoleBinding and RoleBinding with subjets system:anonymous or system:unauthenticated. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="release_channel" type="object">
      ReleaseChannel indicates which release channel a cluster is subscribed to. Release channels are arranged in order of risk. When a cluster is subscribed to a release channel, Google maintains both the master version and the node version. Node auto-upgrade defaults to true and cannot be disabled. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="channel" type="string">
          channel specifies which release channel the cluster is subscribed to. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="resource_labels" type="map(string)">
      The resource labels for the cluster to use to annotate any related Google Compute Engine resources. Optional.
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="resource_usage_export_config" type="object">
      Configuration for exporting cluster resource usages. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="bigquery_destination" type="object">
          Parameters for using BigQuery as the destination of resource usage export. Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="dataset_id" type="string">
              The ID of a BigQuery Dataset. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="consumption_metering_config" type="object">
          Parameters for controlling consumption metering. Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="enabled" type="bool">
              Whether to enable consumption metering for this cluster. If enabled, a second BigQuery table will be created to hold resource consumption records. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="enable_network_egress_metering" type="bool">
          Whether to enable network egress metering for this cluster. If enabled, a daemonset will be created in the cluster to meter network egress traffic. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="rollback_safe_upgrade" type="object">
      RollbackSafeUpgrade is the configuration for the rollback safe upgrade. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="control_plane_soak_duration" type="string">
          Optional. A user-defined period for the cluster remains in the rollbackable state. ex: &#123;seconds: 21600&#125;. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="satisfies_pzi" type="bool">
      Output only. Reserved for future use. Computed by Google Cloud after `ship`.
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="satisfies_pzs" type="bool">
      Output only. Reserved for future use. Computed by Google Cloud after `ship`.
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="schedule_upgrade_config" type="object">
      Configuration for scheduled upgrades on the cluster. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="enabled" type="bool">
          Optional. Whether or not scheduled upgrades are enabled. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="secret_manager_config" type="object">
      SecretManagerConfig is config for secret manager enablement. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="enabled" type="bool">
          Enable/Disable Secret Manager Config. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="rotation_config" type="object">
          RotationConfig is config for secret manager auto rotation. Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="enabled" type="bool">
              Whether the rotation is enabled. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="rotation_interval" type="string">
              The interval between two consecutive rotations. Default rotation interval is 2 minutes. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="secret_sync_config" type="object">
      Configuration for sync Secret Manager secrets as k8s secrets. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="enabled" type="bool">
          Enable/Disable Secret Sync Config. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="rotation_config" type="object">
          SyncRotationConfig is config for secret manager auto rotation. Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="enabled" type="bool">
              Whether the rotation is enabled. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="rotation_interval" type="string">
              The interval between two consecutive rotations. Default rotation interval is 2 minutes. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
          </Expandable>
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="security_posture_config" type="object">
      SecurityPostureConfig defines the flags needed to enable/disable features for the Security Posture API. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="mode" type="string">
          Sets which mode to use for Security Posture features. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="vulnerability_mode" type="string">
          Sets which mode to use for vulnerability scanning. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="self_link" type="string">
      Output only. Server-defined URL for the resource. Computed by Google Cloud after `ship`.
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="services_ipv4_cidr" type="string">
      Output only. The IP address range of the Kubernetes services in this cluster, in [CIDR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classless_Inter-Domain_Routing) notation (e.g. `1.2.3.4/29`). Service addresses are typically put in the last `/16` from the container CIDR. Computed by Google Cloud after `ship`.
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="shielded_nodes" type="object">
      Configuration of Shielded Nodes feature. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="enabled" type="bool">
          Whether Shielded Nodes features are enabled on all nodes in this cluster. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="status" type="string">
      Output only. The current status of this cluster. Computed by Google Cloud after `ship`.
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="status_message" type="string">
      Output only. Deprecated. Use conditions instead. Additional information about the current status of this cluster, if available. Computed by Google Cloud after `ship`.
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="subnetwork" type="string">
      The name of the Google Compute Engine [subnetwork](https://cloud.google.com/compute/docs/subnetworks) to which the cluster is connected. Optional.
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="tpu_ipv4_cidr_block" type="string">
      Output only. The IP address range of the Cloud TPUs in this cluster, in [CIDR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classless_Inter-Domain_Routing) notation (e.g. `1.2.3.4/29`). This field is deprecated due to the deprecation of 2VM TPU. The end of life date for 2VM TPU is 2025-04-25. Computed by Google Cloud after `ship`.
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="user_managed_keys_config" type="object">
      UserManagedKeysConfig holds the resource address to Keys which are used for signing certs and token that are used for communication within cluster. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="aggregation_ca" type="string">
          The Certificate Authority Service caPool to use for the aggregation CA in this cluster. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="cluster_ca" type="string">
          The Certificate Authority Service caPool to use for the cluster CA in this cluster. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="control_plane_disk_encryption_key" type="string">
          The Cloud KMS cryptoKey to use for Confidential Hyperdisk on the control plane nodes. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="control_plane_disk_encryption_key_versions" type="list(string)">
          Output only. All of the versions of the Cloud KMS cryptoKey that are used by Confidential Hyperdisks on the control plane nodes. Computed by Google Cloud after `ship`.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="etcd_api_ca" type="string">
          Resource path of the Certificate Authority Service caPool to use for the etcd API CA in this cluster. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="etcd_peer_ca" type="string">
          Resource path of the Certificate Authority Service caPool to use for the etcd peer CA in this cluster. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="gkeops_etcd_backup_encryption_key" type="string">
          Resource path of the Cloud KMS cryptoKey to use for encryption of internal etcd backups. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="service_account_signing_keys" type="list(string)">
          The Cloud KMS cryptoKeyVersions to use for signing service account JWTs issued by this cluster. Format: `projects/&#123;project&#125;/locations/&#123;location&#125;/keyRings/&#123;keyring&#125;/cryptoKeys/&#123;cryptoKey&#125;/cryptoKeyVersions/&#123;cryptoKeyVersion&#125;` Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="service_account_verification_keys" type="list(string)">
          The Cloud KMS cryptoKeyVersions to use for verifying service account JWTs issued by this cluster. Format: `projects/&#123;project&#125;/locations/&#123;location&#125;/keyRings/&#123;keyring&#125;/cryptoKeys/&#123;cryptoKey&#125;/cryptoKeyVersions/&#123;cryptoKeyVersion&#125;` Optional.
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="vertical_pod_autoscaling" type="object">
      VerticalPodAutoscaling contains global, per-cluster information required by Vertical Pod Autoscaler to automatically adjust the resources of pods controlled by it. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="enabled" type="bool">
          Enables vertical pod autoscaling. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="workload_identity_config" type="object">
      Configuration for the use of Kubernetes Service Accounts in IAM policies. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="workload_pool" type="string">
          The workload pool to attach all Kubernetes service accounts to. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="zone" type="string">
      Output only. The name of the Google Compute Engine [zone](https://cloud.google.com/compute/docs/zones#available) in which the cluster resides. This field is deprecated, use location instead. Computed by Google Cloud after `ship`.
    </ResponseField>
  </Expandable>
</ResponseField>

<ResponseField name="parent" type="string">
  The parent (project and location) where the cluster will be created. Specified in the format `projects/*/locations/*`. Optional.
</ResponseField>

<ResponseField name="project_id" type="string">
  Deprecated. The Google Developers Console [project ID or project number](https://cloud.google.com/resource-manager/docs/creating-managing-projects). This field has been deprecated and replaced by the parent field. Optional.
</ResponseField>

<ResponseField name="zone" type="string">
  Deprecated. The name of the Google Compute Engine [zone](https://cloud.google.com/compute/docs/zones#available) in which the cluster resides. This field has been deprecated and replaced by the parent field. Optional; if omitted, computed by Google Cloud.
</ResponseField>

## Output properties

<ResponseField name="addons_config" type="object">
  Configuration for the addons that can be automatically spun up in the cluster, enabling additional functionality. Computed by Google Cloud after `ship`.
  <Expandable title="properties">
    <ResponseField name="agent_sandbox_config" type="object">
      Configuration for the AgentSandbox addon. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="enabled" type="bool">
          Optional. Whether AgentSandbox is enabled for this cluster. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="cloud_run_config" type="object">
      Configuration options for the Cloud Run feature. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="disabled" type="bool">
          Whether Cloud Run addon is enabled for this cluster. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="load_balancer_type" type="string">
          Which load balancer type is installed for Cloud Run. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="config_connector_config" type="object">
      Configuration options for the Config Connector add-on. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="enabled" type="bool">
          Whether Cloud Connector is enabled for this cluster. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="dns_cache_config" type="object">
      Configuration for NodeLocal DNSCache Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="enabled" type="bool">
          Whether NodeLocal DNSCache is enabled for this cluster. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="gce_persistent_disk_csi_driver_config" type="object">
      Configuration for the Compute Engine PD CSI driver. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="enabled" type="bool">
          Whether the Compute Engine PD CSI driver is enabled for this cluster. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="gcp_filestore_csi_driver_config" type="object">
      Configuration for the Filestore CSI driver. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="enabled" type="bool">
          Whether the Filestore CSI driver is enabled for this cluster. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="gcs_fuse_csi_driver_config" type="object">
      Configuration for the Cloud Storage Fuse CSI driver. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="enabled" type="bool">
          Whether the Cloud Storage Fuse CSI driver is enabled for this cluster. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="gke_backup_agent_config" type="object">
      Configuration for the Backup for GKE Agent. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="enabled" type="bool">
          Whether the Backup for GKE agent is enabled for this cluster. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="high_scale_checkpointing_config" type="object">
      Configuration for the High Scale Checkpointing. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="enabled" type="bool">
          Whether the High Scale Checkpointing is enabled for this cluster. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="horizontal_pod_autoscaling" type="object">
      Configuration options for the horizontal pod autoscaling feature, which increases or decreases the number of replica pods a replication controller has based on the resource usage of the existing pods. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="disabled" type="bool">
          Whether the Horizontal Pod Autoscaling feature is enabled in the cluster. When enabled, it ensures that metrics are collected into Stackdriver Monitoring. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="http_load_balancing" type="object">
      Configuration options for the HTTP (L7) load balancing controller addon, which makes it easy to set up HTTP load balancers for services in a cluster. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="disabled" type="bool">
          Whether the HTTP Load Balancing controller is enabled in the cluster. When enabled, it runs a small pod in the cluster that manages the load balancers. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="kubernetes_dashboard" type="object">
      Configuration for the Kubernetes Dashboard. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="disabled" type="bool">
          Whether the Kubernetes Dashboard is enabled for this cluster. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="lustre_csi_driver_config" type="object">
      Configuration for the Lustre CSI driver. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="disable_multi_nic" type="bool">
          When set to true, this disables multi-NIC support for the Lustre CSI driver. By default, GKE enables multi-NIC support, which allows the Lustre CSI driver to automatically detect and configure all suitable network interfaces on a node to maximize I/O performance for demanding workloads. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="enable_legacy_lustre_port" type="bool">
          If set to true, the Lustre CSI driver will install Lustre kernel modules using port 6988. This serves as a workaround for a port conflict with the gke-metadata-server. This field is required ONLY under the following conditions: 1. The GKE node version is older than 1.33.2-gke.4655000. 2. You're connecting to a Lustre instance that has the 'gke-support-enabled' flag. Deprecated: This flag is no longer required as of GKE node version 1.33.2-gke.4655000, unless you are connecting to a Lustre instance that has the `gke-support-enabled` flag. Optional.
